Each function raises `cli.errors.RejectedError` (exit 3) on violation and otherwise returns the validated value.\n\n## Interface\n\n| Function | Returns | Rule |\n|----------|---------|------|\n| `validate_code(value: str) -> str` | the value | 1 to 12 characters from `A-Z` and `0-9` |\n| `validate_name(value: str) -> str` | the value | 1 to 40 characters, no comma, no newline (also used for descriptions) |\n| `validate_memo(value: str) -> str` | the value | 0 to 60 characters, no comma, no newline |\n| `parse_amount(text: str) -> int` | integer cents | matches `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]`, at most two decimals, never zero |\n| `validate_date(text: str) -> str` | the value | real calendar date written `YYYY-MM-DD` |\n| `parse_txn_id(text: str) -> int` | the integer | a non-empty string of decimal digits |\n\n## Rules\n\n- Amount parsing uses integer arithmetic on the digit strings only. No `float` or `Decimal`-free shortcut through floating point is permitted at any layer.\n- `.5`, `5.`, `1.234`, `+5`, `1e3`, `0`, `0.0` and `0.00` are rejected.\n- A date must match the `YYYY-MM-DD` shape exactly and be a real calendar date; `2026-02-30` is rejected.\n- A transaction id with a sign, a decimal point, whitespace or no digits is rejected with exit 3.\n- Validation reads no database and writes nothing.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC validation-codes ===\nIntent: Codes of 1 to 12 characters from A-Z and 0-9 are returned unchanged and every other shape is rejected with exit code 3.\n\nfrom cli.errors import RejectedError\nfrom ledgerlib import validation\n\ngood = \"DRAWER1\"\nlongest = \"A\" * 12\nassert validation.validate_code(good) == good\nassert validation.validate_code(longest) == longest\nfor bad in [\"\", \"A\" * 13, \"drawer1\", \"AB-C\", \"A B\", \"AB,C\"]:\n    try:\n        validation.validate_code(bad)\n    except RejectedError as exc:\n        assert exc.exit_code == 3\n    else:\n        raise AssertionError(\"accepted bad code\")\n=== END AC validation-codes ===\n\n=== AC validation-names-memos ===\nIntent: Names are 1 to 40 characters and memos 0 to 60 characters, neither may contain a comma or newline, and violations exit 3.\n\nfrom cli.errors import RejectedError\nfrom ledgerlib import validation\n\nname = \"Front Drawer\"\nassert validation.validate_name(name) == name\nassert validation.validate_name(\"N\" * 40) == \"N\" * 40\nassert validation.validate_memo(\"\") == \"\"\nassert validation.validate_memo(\"m\" * 60) == \"m\" * 60\n\nbad_names = [\"\", \"N\" * 41, \"a,b\", \"a\" + chr(10) + \"b\"]\nfor bad in bad_names:\n    try:\n        validation.validate_name(bad)\n    except RejectedError as exc:\n        assert exc.exit_code == 3\n    else:\n        raise AssertionError(\"accepted bad name\")\n\nbad_memos = [\"m\" * 61, \"a,b\", \"a\" + chr(10) + \"b\"]\nfor bad in bad_memos:\n    try:\n        validation.validate_memo(bad)\n    except RejectedError as exc:\n        assert exc.exit_code == 3\n    else:\n        raise AssertionError(\"accepted bad memo\")\n=== END AC validation-names-memos ===\n\n=== AC validation-amounts ===\nIntent: Amounts parse to exact integer cents with at most two decimals and zero, malformed or over-precise amounts are rejected with exit 3.\n\nfrom cli.errors import RejectedError\nfrom ledgerlib import validation\n\ncases = [\n    (\"12.50\", 1250),\n    (\"-12.50\", -1250),\n   assert isinstance(got, int) and not isinstance(got, bool)\n    assert got == cents\n\nfor bad in [\"0\", \"0.00\", \"-0\", \"1.234\", \"abc\", \"1.\", \".5\", \"+5\", \"1e3\", \"\", \"1,5\"]:\n    try:\n        validation.parse_amount(bad)\n    except RejectedError as exc:\n        assert exc.exit_code == 3\n    else:\n        raise AssertionError(\"accepted bad amount \" + bad)\n=== END AC validation-amounts ===\n\n=== AC validation-dates ===\nIntent: Only real calendar dates written YYYY-MM-DD are accepted, so a nonexistent day or a different shape exits 3.\n\nfrom cli.errors import message wording is not part of the contract; only the exit code is.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-LedgerRules.md ===\n# FEATURE: Ledger Rules\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | Compute account views and projected balances, enforce the non-negative balance rule, and allocate transaction ids. |\n| Depends On | DATABASE.md, FEATURE-CsvOutput.md, FEATURE-Validation.md |\n| Provides | ledgerlib.rules |\n| Consumes | persistence, ledgerlib.csvout |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledgerlib/rules.py` owns the business rules every write command shares: an account's transaction count and balance, the projected balance after a set of changes, the rule that no balance goes below zero, and transaction id allocation. All reads go through `db.<table>.<operation>(...)`; balances are computed in Python from list rows in integer cents.\n\n## Interface\n\n| Function | Behavior |\n|----------|----------|\n| `account_view(db, code)` | Returns an object with `account`, `name`, `transactions` (count) and `balance` (integer cents), or `None` when the account is absent. |\n| `balances_after(db, changes)` | `changes` is a list of `(account_code, delta_cents)` pairs. Returns a dict mapping each affected account to its balance after every change in the list is applied. |\n| `require_non_negative(db, changes)` | Raises `cli.errors.RejectedError` (exit 3) when any account in the projected result would be below zero; returns `None` otherwise. Writes nothing. |\n| `next_txn_id(db)` | Returns the largest stored `txn_id` plus one, or `1` when there are no transactions. |\n\n## Rules\n\n- The balance check is evaluated on each account's final balance after the whole change set, not change by change, so a set that dips below zero and recovers is accepted.\n- A balance of exactly zero is accepted.\n- Callers read `next_txn_id` and write the row inside one database transaction so two concurrent posts never receive the same id.\n- Every check happens before the caller's first write; these functions perform no writes.\n- No floating point is used for money.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-002` — read: get an account by code - supplies: `account` - returns: `account`, `name`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-013` — read: list transactions, optionally for one account - supplies: `account` - returns: `txn_id`, `date`, `account`, `category`, `amount`, `memo`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC rules-account-view ===\nIntent: The account view reports the transaction count and the integer-cent balance computed from stored rows, and an absent account yields none.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledgerlib import rules\n\nacct = \"ZZRLV1\"\nmissing = \"ZZRLVNONE\"\ncat = \"ZZRLCAT1\"\nfirst = 930001\nsecond = 930002\nplus = 10000\nminus = -2550\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=acct, name=\"Rules View\")\n    db.categories.create(category=cat, description=\"Rules View Cat\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=first, date=\"2026-05-01\", account=acct, category=cat, amount=plus, memo=\"a\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=second, date=\"2026-05-02\", account=acct, category=cat, amount=minus, memo=\"b\")\n    view = rules.account_view(db, acct)\n    assert view is not None\n    assert view.account == acct\n    assert view.transactions == 2\n    assert view.balance == plus + minus\n    assert isinstance(view.balance, int)\n    assert rules.account_view(db, missing) is None\n=== END AC rules-account-view ===\n\n=== AC rules-balances-after ===\nIntent: The projection applies the whole change set to current balances for every affected account without writing anything.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledgerlib import rules\n\na = \"ZZRLB1\"\nb = \"ZZRLB2\"\ncat = \"ZZRLCAT2\"\nstart_a = 5000\nstart_b = 700\ndelta = 1200\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=a, name=\"Rules Proj A\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=b, name=\"Rules Proj B\")\n    db.categories.create(category=cat, description=\"Rules Proj Cat\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=930011, date=\"2026-05-01\", account=a, category=cat, amount=start_a, memo=\"a\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=930012, date=\"2026-05-01\", account=b, category=cat, amount=start_b, memo=\"b\")\n    before = len(db.transactions.list())\n    result = rules.balances_after(db, [(a, -delta), (b, delta)])\n    assert result[a] == start_a - delta\n    assert result[b] == start_b + delta\n    assert len(db.transactions.list()) == before\n=== END AC rules-balances-after ===\n\n=== AC rules-require-non-negative ===\nIntent: A change set leaving any balance below zero is rejected with exit 3 while exactly zero and a recovering set are accepted, and nothing is written.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.errors import RejectedError\nfrom ledgerlib import rules\n\nacct = \"ZZRLN1\"\ncat = \"ZZRLCAT3\"\nstart = 5000\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=acct, name=\"Rules Neg\")\n    db.categories.create(category=cat, description=\"Rules Neg Cat\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=930021, date=\"2026-05-01\", rules.require_non_negative(db, [(acct, -8000), (acct, 4000)]) is None\n\n    try:\n        rules.require_non_negative(db, [(acct, -(start + 1))])\n    except RejectedError as exc:\n        assert rules-next-txn-id ===\nIntent: The next id is the largest stored id plus one, or one for an empty table, derived here from the stored rows.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledgerlib import rules\n\nacct = \"ZZRLI1\"\ncat = \"ZZRLCAT4\"\nhigh = 940500\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=acct, name=\"Rules Id\")\n which prepares an empty ledger and removes existing rows when rerun. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-DatabaseSelection.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | ledger init |\n| Consumes | ledgerlib.config, persistence |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledger init` makes the configured database ready and empty. It creates the parent directory of the SQLite file when missing, opens the database through `open_database` (which provisions the declared tables idempotently), then removes every row, children first: transactions, then categories, then accounts. It writes nothing to standard output and exits 0.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `cli/commands/init.py` exposes `run(args) -> list[str]` and returns no lines.\n- Any argument is a usage error (exit 2).\n- A PostgreSQL DSN or unreachable database exits 1 with nothing on standard output.\n- Clearing uses the declared `clear` operations with the lowest possible bound (`min_txn_id` of a value below every id, empty-string bounds for codes). No driver import and no DDL outside the persistence library.\n- Rerunning on a populated ledger removes every row, leaving all three tables empty.\n- Creating a missing parent directory is covered by the project's own pytest suite, which owns temporary database locations.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-006` — write: clear accounts from a code upward - supplies: `min_account`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-010` — write: clear categories from a code upward - supplies: `min_category`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-016` — write: clear transactions from an id upward - supplies: `min_txn_id`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC init-clears-rows ===\nIntent: Init exits 0 with empty standard output and leaves every table empty after rows were seeded through the persistence interface.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nacct = \"ZZINI1\"\ncat = \"ZZINCAT1\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=acct, name=\"Init Seed\")\n len(db.transactions.list()) >= 1\n\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 0\n    assert out == \"\"\n\n    assert db.transactions.list() == []\n    assert db.categories.list() == []\n    assert db.accounts.list() == []\n    assert db.accounts.get(account=acct) is None\n=== END AC init-clears-rows ===\n\n=== AC init-rerun ===\nIntent: Running ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-LoadAccounts.md ===\n# FEATURE: Load Accounts\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | Implement `ledger load accounts <csv>` as an all-or-nothing bulk load. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-DatabaseSelection.md, FEATURE-Validation.md, FEATURE-CsvOutput.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | ledger load accounts |\n| Consumes | ledgerlib.config, ledgerlib.validation, ledgerlib.csvout |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli/commands/load.py` owns `ledger load <table> <csv>` with a per-table handler registry; an unknown table is a usage error (exit 2). This story adds the `accounts` handler. The whole file is accepted or none of it is stored.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The header must be exactly `account,name`; any other header is rejected with exit 3.\n- Every data row must have the header's field count; each code and name is validated with `ledgerlib.validation`.\n- A duplicate code within the file, or one already stored, is rejected with exit 3.\n- Every check completes before the first write. Writes happen in one database transaction, so a rejected file stores nothing.\n- A file that cannot be read exits 1 with nothing on standard output.\n- A header-only file loads zero rows, prints `accounts,0`, and exits 0.\n- Output is `table,loaded` followed by `accounts,<rows>`.\n- Missing arguments or extra arguments are a usage error (exit 2).\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-001` — write: create an account - supplies: `account`, `name` - returns: `account`, `name`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-002` — read: get an account by code - supplies: `account` - returns: `account`, `name`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC load-accounts-success ===\nIntent: A valid accounts file stores every row, prints the loaded count, and the rows read back through the persistence interface.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nrecords = [(\"ZZLA1\", \"Load One\"), (\"ZZLA2\", \"Load Two\"), (\"ZZLA3\", \"Load Three\")]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:\n    path = os.path.join(d, \"accounts.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as fh:\n        w = csv.writer(fh, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\n        w.writerow([\"account\", \"name\"])\n        w.writerows(records)\n    with db_session() as db:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", \"accounts\", path)\n        print(out)\n        print(err)\n        assert code == 0\n        rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n        assert rows[0] == [\"table\", \"loaded\"]\n        assert rows[1] == [\"accounts\", str(len(records))]\n        assert len(rows) == 2\n        for acct, name in records:\n            got = db.accounts.get(account=acct)\n            assert got is not None\n            assert got.name == name\n=== END AC load-accounts-success ===\n\n=== AC load-accounts-header-only ===\nIntent: A file with a valid header and no data rows loads zero rows, prints a    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n        assert rows == [[\"table\", \"loaded\"], [\"accounts\", \"0\"]]\n        assert len(db.accounts.list()) == before\n=== END AC BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Validation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | Validate codes, names, memos, amounts, dates and transaction ids per the ledger rules. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliEntry.md |\n| Provides | ledgerlib.validation |\n| Consumes | cli.main |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command validates its inputs before its first write. `ledgerlib/validation.py` is the single owner of the field rules in `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` Rules 3 to 6 and the transaction id rule. Each function raises `cli.errors.RejectedError` (exit 3) on violation and otherwise returns the validated value.\n\n## Interface\n\n| Function | Returns | Rule |\n|----------|---------|------|\n| `validate_code(value: str) -> str` | the value | 1 to 12 characters from `A-Z` and `0-9` |\n| `validate_name(value: str) -> str` | the value | 1 to 40 characters, no comma, no newline (also used for descriptions) |\n| `validate_memo(value: str) -> str` | the value | 0 to 60 characters, no comma, no newline |\n| `parse_amount(text: str) -> int` | integer cents | matches `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]`, at most two decimals, never zero |\n| `validate_date(text: str) -> str` | the value | real calendar date written `YYYY-MM-DD` |\n| `parse_txn_id(text: str) -> int` | the integer | a non-empty string of decimal digits |\n\n## Rules\n\n- Amount parsing uses integer arithmetic on the digit strings only. No `float` or `Decimal`-free shortcut through floating point is permitted at any layer.\n- `.5`, `5.`, `1.234`, `+5`, `1e3`, `0`, `0.0` and `0.00` are rejected.\n- A date must match the `YYYY-MM-DD` shape exactly and be a real calendar date; `2026-02-30` is rejected.\n- A transaction id with a sign, a decimal point, whitespace or no digits is rejected with exit 3.\n- Validation reads no database and writes nothing.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC validation-codes ===\nIntent: Codes of 1 to 12 characters from A-Z |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | Compute account views and projected balances, enforce the non-negative balance rule, and allocate transaction ids. |\n| Depends On | DATABASE.md, FEATURE-CsvOutput.md, FEATURE-Validation.md |\n| Provides | ledgerlib.rules |\n| Consumes | persistence, ledgerlib.csvout |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledgerlib/rules.py` owns the business rules every write command shares: an account's transaction count and balance, the projected balance after a set of changes, the rule that no balance goes below zero, and transaction id allocation. All reads go through `db.<table>.<operation>(...)`; balances are computed in Python from list rows in integer cents.\n\n## Interface\n\n| Function | Behavior |\n|----------|----------|\n| `account_view(db, code)` | Returns an object with `account`, `name`, `transactions` (count) and `balance` (integer cents), or `None` when the account is absent. |\n| `balances_after(db, changes)` | `changes` is a list of `(account_code, delta_cents)` pairs. Returns a dict mapping each affected account to its balance after every change in the list is applied. |\n| `require_non_negative(db, changes)` | Raises `cli.errors.RejectedError` (exit 3) when any account in the projected result would be below zero; returns `None` otherwise. Writes nothing. |\n| `next_txn_id(db)` | Returns the largest stored `txn_id` plus one, or `1` when there are no transactions. |\n\n## Rules\n\n- The balance check is evaluated on each account's final balance after the whole change set, not change by change, so a set that dips below zero and recovers is accepted.\n- A balance of exactly zero is accepted.\n- Callers read `next_txn_id` and write the row inside one database transaction so two concurrent posts never receive the same id.\n- Every check happens before the caller's first write; these functions perform no writes.\n- No floating point is used for money.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-002` — read: get an account by code - supplies: `account` - returns: `account`, `name`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-013` — read: list transactions, optionally for one account - supplies: `account` - returns: `txn_id`, `date`, `account`, `category`, `amount`, `memo`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC rules-account-view ===\nIntent: The account view reports the transaction count and the integer-cent balance computed from stored rows, and an absent account yields none.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom ledgerlib import rules\n\nacct = \"ZZRLV1\"\nmissing = \"ZZRLVNONE\"\ncat = \"ZZRLCAT1\"\nfirst = 930001\nsecond = 930002\nplus = 10000\nminus = -2550\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=acct, name=\"Rules View\")\n    db.categories.create(category=cat, description=\"Rules View Cat\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=first, date=\"2026-05-01\", account=acct, category=cat, amount=plus, memo=\"a\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=second, date=\"2026-05-02\", account=acct, category=cat, amount=minus, memo=\"b\")\n    view = rules.account_view(db, acct)\n    assert view is not None\n    assert view.account == acct\n    assert view.transactions == 2\n    assert view.balance == plus + minus\n    assert isinstance(view.balance, int)\n    assert rules.account_view(db, missing) is None\n=== END AC rules-account-view ===\n\n=== AC rules-balances-after ===\nIntent: The projection applies the whole change \"ZZRLCAT2\"\nstart_a = 5000\nstart_b = 700\ndelta = 1200\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.accounts.create(account=a, name=\"Rules Proj A\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=b, name=\"Rules Proj rules-require-non-negative ===\nIntent: A change set leaving any balance below zero is rejected with exit 3 while exactly zero and a recovering set are accepted, and nothing is written.\n\nfrom which prepares an empty ledger and removes existing rows when rerun. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-DatabaseSelection.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | ledger init |\n| Consumes | ledgerlib.config, persistence |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledger init` makes the configured database ready and empty. It creates the parent directory of the SQLite file when missing, opens the database through `open_database` (which provisions the declared tables idempotently), then removes every row, children first: transactions, then categories, then accounts. It writes nothing to standard output and exits 0.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `cli/commands/init.py` exposes `run(args) -> list[str]` and returns no lines.\n- Any argument is a usage error (exit 2).\n- A PostgreSQL DSN or unreachable database exits 1 with nothing on standard output.\n- Clearing uses the declared `clear` operations with the lowest possible bound (`min_txn_id` of a value below every id, empty-string bounds for codes). No driver import and no DDL outside the persistence library.\n- Rerunning on a populated ledger removes every row, leaving all three tables empty.\n- Creating a missing parent directory is covered by the project's own pytest suite, which owns temporary database locations.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-006` — write: clear accounts from a code upward - supplies: `min_account`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-010` — write: clear categories from a code upward - supplies: `min_category`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-016` — write: clear transactions from an id upward - supplies: `min_txn_id`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC init-clears-rows ===\nIntent: Init exits 0 with empty standard output and leaves every table empty after rows were seeded through the persistence interface.\n\nfrom len(db.transactions.list()) >= 1\n\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(\"init\")\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 0\n    assert out == \"\"\n\n    assert db.transactions.list() == []\n    assert db.categories.list() == []\n    assert db.accounts.list() == []\n    assert db.accounts.get(account=acct) is None\n=== END AC init-clears-rows ===\n\n=== AC init-rerun ===\nIntent: Running ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-LoadAccounts.md ===\n# FEATURE: Load Accounts\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | Implement `ledger load accounts <csv>` as an all-or-nothing bulk load. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-DatabaseSelection.md, FEATURE-Validation.md, FEATURE-CsvOutput.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | ledger load accounts |\n| Consumes | ledgerlib.config, ledgerlib.validation, ledgerlib.csvout |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`cli/commands/load.py` owns `ledger load <table> <csv>` with a per-table handler registry; an unknown table is a usage error (exit 2). This story adds the `accounts` handler. The whole file is accepted or none of it is stored.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The header must be exactly `account,name`; any other header is rejected with exit 3.\n- Every data row must have the header's field count; each code and name is validated with `ledgerlib.validation`.\n- A duplicate code within the file, or one already stored, is rejected with exit 3.\n- Every check completes before the first write. Writes happen in one database transaction, so a rejected file stores nothing.\n- A file that cannot be read exits 1 with nothing on standard output.\n- A header-only file loads zero rows, prints `accounts,0`, and exits 0.\n- Output is `table,loaded` followed by `accounts,<rows>`.\n- Missing arguments or extra arguments are a usage error (exit 2).\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-001` — write: create an account - supplies: `account`, `name` - returns: `account`, `name`\n- `FEATURE-LEDGER-002` — read: get an account by code - supplies: `account` - returns: `account`, `name`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC load-accounts-success ===\nIntent: A valid accounts file stores every row, prints the loaded count, and the rows read back through the persistence interface.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nrecords = [(\"ZZLA1\", \"Load One\"), (\"ZZLA2\", \"Load Two\"), (\"ZZLA3\", \"Load Three\")]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:\n    path = os.path.join(d, \"accounts.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as fh:\n        w = csv.writer(fh, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\n        w.writerow([\"account\", \"name\"])\n        w.writerows(records)\n    with db_session() as db:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", \"accounts\", path)\n        print(out)\n        print(err)\n        assert code == 0\n        rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n        assert rows[0] == [\"table\", \"loaded\"]\n        assert rows[1] == [\"accounts\", str(len(records))]\n        assert len(rows) == 2\n        for acct, name in records:\n            got = db.accounts.get(account=acct)\n            assert got is not None\n            assert got.name == name\n=== END AC load-accounts-success ===\n\n=== AC load-accounts-header-only ===\nIntent: A file with a valid header and no data rows loads zero rows, prints a
